# Post-review list — every family with scores, disposition and refuter verdict

Families are groups of cards making the same claim (clustered by Sonnet, organising only); every member card inherits the family's scores and disposition. Scores are 0–3 per dimension from two Opus reviewers (A = benefit-seeker, B = cost-and-novelty skeptic) in the order evidence / transferability / novelty / benefit / cost-risk. A refuter (Opus, xhigh, fresh context, default verdict refuted) ran on every family at least one reviewer marked adopt or trial, up to the per-group cap; "unrefuted (over cap)" families keep both reviewers' dispositions and no final verdict.

## G1 — W1 + L2 — CLAUDE.md length, context budget, compaction (211 families, 444 cards)

| Family | Final | Claim | Cards | A: e/t/n/b/c → disp | B: e/t/n/b/c → disp | Refuter | One line |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G1-F010 | **adopt** | Delete from CLAUDE.md anything a linter, formatter or hook can enforce; express every checkable rule as a linter so agents fix it themselves. | 2: C1201, C0500 | 1/3/0/2/3 → already-done | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed. Deleting CLAUDE.md rules is refuted because D11 keeps every rule and the pre-commit hook skip in HR §4 still stands. Most rules that can be linted already are, through import-linter, ESLint, alembic check, the P1 head check and P5b-2's dependency-cruiser. What survives is three cheap CI checks for the remaining traps, each added in a phase that already touches that config: ruff TID251 banning hmac.compare_digest (with ignores for core/security.py and identity/security.py), plus a mobile-job JSON assertion that rejects an empty eas.json env value and the Sentry plugin in app.json. Merge this with F011 if it covers the same traps; the benefit is on L3/W2 backpressure, not W1 length. |
| G1-F011 | **adopt** | Mechanise project-specific traps and agent-hostile constructs as scripts, lint bans or guardrails in one gate, rather than as prose rules. | 3: C0127, C0393, C0428 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 2/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed to three cheap, history-backed CI checks: an eas.json check for empty env values, an app.json check that bans the Sentry plugin unless SENTRY_DISABLE_AUTO_UPLOAD is set, and a Ruff banned-api ban on hmac.compare_digest. The mobile checks also run locally as an npm script. The CLAUDE.md prose stays until D11 trims it, and the useEffect ban and the deploy-from-root check are dropped. |
| G1-F046 | **adopt** | Keep secrets out of agent context: append to .env without reading it and reference test credentials by variable name, never in commands, screenshots or chat. | 2: C0067, C0225 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/2/1/1/3 → skip | narrowed → adopt | The W1/L2 framing, the CLAUDE.md placement, the generic CONTEXT-block line and the test-credential half are refuted. A smaller rule survives, aimed at the phases that handle secrets. In the P0a, P3b, P3c and P4a briefs, secret values are passed by variable name (PGPASSWORD for pg_dump; $CF_ACCESS_CLIENT_SECRET for curl), committed records name the variable and never the value, and agents never Read or cat a .env. Separately, the owner decides whether to add a project-level deny rule on Read of .env files: it is enforced by the tool and costs no always-loaded context. |
| G1-F085 | **adopt** | Tell-tale of the dumb zone is the model flailing on a failing test (extreme attempts, claiming preexisting failure); write state to a file and restart fresh. | 4: C0494, C0853, C0904, C0877 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed: skip the dumb-zone restart trigger. It rests on anecdote, Fable cannot see subagent flailing mid-run, and fresh-per-brief subagents, HR-C1 and HR-C2 already cover it. Adopt only a one-clause tightening of §0 Routing: a "preexisting/unrelated" reason for a new skip, xfail or red test must show the same failure on the base SHA. If it cannot, the work is rejected and Fable re-briefs a fresh agent from the PR journal. |
| G1-F004 | **trial** | Keep CLAUDE.md for what is always true (identity, map, stack; ~90%+ relevance) bare, and give each conditional block one narrow trigger because a broad condition matches everything. | 4: C1198, C1199, C1209, C0816 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/1/3 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed: this survives only as the authoring rule for F001's wrapped arm, and only if E1 adds oblique probes that test for missed triggers. The "keep always-true content bare" half is already the harness's position under KS-7/D11 ("Do NOT assume" unchanged, every rule kept). Wrapping does not shorten the 390-line file. The evidence is asserted, with no data. FixFlow's invariants are coupled across slices, so a too-narrow trigger, not a too-broad one, is the real risk. |
| G1-F016 | **trial** | Re-validate CLAUDE.md, skills and prompts at every model release, because harness pieces expire as models improve. | 3: C0640, C0508, C0467 | 1/2/3/2/2 → trial | 1/2/2/1/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Survives only as a regression check on CLAUDE.md invariant adherence when a model changes, attached to E1 (same frozen SHA, old and new model side by side, repeated runs). Refuted parts: the cold-resume rerun (wrong instrument, pinned to Sonnet), per-release skill and prompt evals (reopens M00–M06), and "remove expired pieces" (no evidence, and closed off by KS-7's keep-every-rule-sentence trim). |
| G1-F022 | **trial** | Scope instructions to code regions: directory-scoped CLAUDE.md and glob-scoped rules load conditionally when files in that path are touched, and memories can be tied to a region to check staleness. | 3: C0283, C0977, C1278 | 2/3/3/3/2 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The trial should split off only material that applies to one runtime, keep every cross-runtime rule and a pointer index in the root, and test briefs and diff-only reviewers alongside implementers. The mechanism works here: I observed a nested CLAUDE.md attach in a workflow subagent. But it loads only on the Read tool, so Fable's briefs and diff-only reviewers lose whatever moves. |
| G1-F036 | **trial** | Names and descriptions of every skill, subagent and MCP tool are injected each context and eat the instruction budget; keep descriptions short and hide user-only skills with disable-model-invocation. | 2: C1270, C1271 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/2/2/2 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Narrowed to a trial. The flag half is refuted: per the vendor docs it blocks subagent preload, which 12 of the 13 skills rely on, so it only fits a future owner-typed slash command that no agent preloads. The MCP half is already deferred. What survives is a trial trim of the ~24k chars of skill and agent descriptions, which is an L2 gain only and needs a routing check before adoption. |
| G1-F042 | **trial** | Make context use visible (a /context breakdown, an always-visible meter, model-aware warnings, tracked token counts) so budget decisions use a number. | 3: C1179, C0262, C0984 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The meter, the /context breakdown and the warnings already exist natively (the desktop composer and get_usage), and the W1 number is now known: about 10k tokens (1%). What survives is one line in the closing message: Fable calls get_usage("self") at phase close, with no owner transcription and no new tooling. That gives HR-C4's 500k trigger its missing sensor. |
| G1-F048 | **trial** | Default check scripts should run quiet so only failures print, since passing-test lines are wasted context. | 2: C0069, C1579 | 1/3/3/3/2 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The backend half is already done: Appendix A's g() wrapper keeps full logs on disk and prints only exit lines, using pytest -q. What survives as a trial is extending that same wrapper, with a pass/fail/skip count line, to the native web and mobile gates, and only if a single green-run measurement shows their output is worth cutting. The ~75k smart-zone figure is refuted for this harness. |
| G1-F069 | **trial** | Design docs should list patterns to follow with source paths, and surface applicable codebase patterns for humans to choose old versus new, so models do not default to patterns the codebase avoids. | 3: C0043, C1220, C0981 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed: only one thing survives. Sonnet implementation briefs get one field naming 1-3 existing files to mirror, which extends ORCH §5.3 and can be filled by the fixflow-explorer locator. It does nothing for W1 (CLAUDE.md is untouched and the trim stays gated), and the human old-vs-new choice step is dropped. The evidence is asserted or anecdotal, so it is a trial with a blind paired probe, not the 2-vs-2 PR count. |
| G1-F098 | **trial** | A monolithic multi-step prompt of 50-85 instructions had steps skipped about half the time; splitting it into prompts under 40 instructions each fixed the skipping. | 7: C1106, C1118, C1311, C1332, C1335, C1435 … | 1/3/3/3/2 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The evidence is one confounded anecdote: no adherence rate after the split, the 40 cutoff is unfounded, and the fix also added human-invoked stops between prompts. So G1-A's "count §0, split if over 40" is refuted. What survives is G1-B's version: audit the §0 obligations in P1 and P2a, and only if a step is missed, move that moment into a short checklist in the fixflow-orchestration skill (not §0). No W1 or W2 credit. |
| G1-F157 | **trial** | Loading many MCP servers/tool definitions eats the context budget (measured about 5.7k tokens for one MCP), so tool surface should be chosen per need. | 3: C1601, C1772, C1776 | 2/2/3/1/3 → trial | 2/2/0/1/3 → already-done | narrowed → trial | Refuted as stated (MCP servers crowd the phase window; adopt a loadout policy): most MCP schemas are already deferred, the window is about 1M, and §6 shows tool results fill it, not definitions. A smaller version survives: on high-volume workflow subagents, a whitelisted tool set (as fixflow-explorer already uses) may cut a measured ~64k first-turn prompt that is re-read on every call. Trial it with one /context reading plus one A/B workflow; install nothing. |
| G1-F159 | **trial** | Keep context utilisation under roughly 40% (range 30-60%) and restart before hitting limits. | 5: C1690, C1735, C1751, C1777, C1834 | 1/2/2/2/3 → trial | 1/1/1/1/3 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The 40% budget is anecdotal, was set on 200k windows, and HR-C1, HR-C4 and the phase sizing already cover its intent. Only a near-free instrument survives: log Fable's peak context and the token position of each corrected Fable decision for 5 phases, to fire HR-C4 and to test whether high fill degrades the judge. |
| G1-F170 | **trial** | Repeatedly correcting a derailed agent builds a bad trajectory; restart quickly after a fixed number of failed attempts instead of steering in place. | 2: C1613, C1854 | 1/3/2/1/3 → trial | 1/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | This survives only as a narrow trial. The rule is one §0 sentence (not in CLAUDE.md, so W1 does not grow): after two failed corrections on the same box, commit and push the WIP, write what failed into the journal's "ruled out" section, and resume in a new session rather than a fork. A failed subagent gets a new brief in a fresh agent rather than more messages to the same one. If the box fails a third time after the restart, it goes to the plan-wrong path, not to another restart. Drop C1854's 10/30-minute timebox. The two reviewers split between trial and adopt; I side with trial. |

| Family | Final | Claim | Cards | A: e/t/n/b/c → disp | B: e/t/n/b/c → disp | Refuter | One line |

| G2-F015 | **adopt** | Verification claims must cite the command run and its observed result (separate tests read from checks executed; check stored end state; verify by executable derivation), not agent assertion. | 3: C0023, C0223, C0216 | 1/3/1/2/3 → already-done | 1/3/1/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed: C0216 is already planned (P1's CI head check) and C0223 is already practice (integration tests against real Postgres, post-deploy checks). The pass-after half is already proven by CI being green on the PR. What survives is one clause in the §0 reviewer brief: each fail-before line cites the command run on the base SHA and its observed result, or is marked 'read, not run'. It costs almost nothing and makes that claim checkable, but it is not hook-enforced. |
| G2-F018 | **adopt** | When workflow documents conflict, a fixed precedence order applies (latest decision wins; user's latest instruction overrides all), stated up front. | 3: C0032, C0080, C0213 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/2/1/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed: adopt one sentence in ship-plan §0, never in CLAUDE.md (W1), split by domain rather than "latest wins": for this phase's work, journal owner correction > plan on main (after the existing changed-section re-read) > Plan @ sha > cited design note; for current stack facts and invariants CLAUDE.md + code win, as they already say; a stricter plan rule beats a looser CLAUDE.md invariant; any other conflict is a stop plus a journal entry, not a guess. It helps W3 only. |

What survives is a one-row swap in CLAUDE.md's doc map: move "Start here" from HANDOFF-2026-07-28 to SHIP-PLAN §0, in the PR that merges #182 or in P1's PR. |

What survives is not a hook and not an alembic rule: a single `permissions.deny` block with Bash and PowerShell forms of `railway up` and `railway run` (optionally `git push --force`). It cheaply makes a locally recorded outage trap mechanical, which still matters for the Railway stub during the drain window. It does nothing for W2. |

Fatal on .claude/skills/*/SKILL.md frontmatter (name equals the folder, description present and at most 1024 chars) and on agent frontmatter fields. Extend the CLAUDE.md Alembic-head check to flag a stale head literal in .claude/skills. Path citations containing a slash are warn-only. Drop the RPI numbered-phase, byte-identical-mirror and naive whole-file link checks. |

Without that, the only test of W3 can pass by reading the key, since P1 itself points to it. The no-history raw checkout and the replay-for-effort benchmark do not survive. |

This helps W3 only, not W2. The config-epoch half is already done, and a hook or script version would re-adopt the HR §4 skip. |

The broader "verify every checkout, propose pulls" form is unnecessary here. |

That duplicates SHIP-PLAN §0's six-question close, which is already an early-loaded, late-checked, visible test of instruction-following. A per-turn canary is weaker: the model can keep it alive by copying its own earlier replies. It also does not measure whether the routine was followed (W2) or whether length hurts adherence (W1), and placed in CLAUDE.md it would make that file longer and reach every subagent. Its only support is an informal demo relayed second-hand, so the G2-B skip stands. |

The citation rule would apply to zero current records and does nothing for W4 or L1 (OrcaBot tested only effort=high). Fold them into F066's transfer score; there is no standalone disposition. |

| G3-F090 | **skip** | Cache each LLM call keyed by input hash to a JSON file so changing step five reruns without paying for steps one to four. | 1: C0279 | 1/1/1/0/2 → skip | 1/1/2/0/2 → skip | — | reviewers agree: A hand-built hash cache adds Windows file plumbing to scripts that are run once and, by design, not saved. |

| G3-F097 | **skip** | Bash is considered harmful as the agent execution environment and code mode should not be the default because models are not yet trained on it; use it only for shaped problems. | 2: C1067, C0422 | 0/1/1/0/3 → skip | 0/1/1/0/2 → skip | — | reviewers agree: A prediction, not evidence, and the harness relies on Git Bash recipes that work. |

| G3-F099 | **skip** | For rich-document editing, have the model write a markdown edit and a small model convert it into a code snippet because model editing tools are poor. | 1: C0558 | 1/0/1/0/2 → skip | 1/0/1/0/2 → skip | — | reviewers agree: There is no rich-document editing in this program. |

That means real cost and injection risk for a pattern no FixFlow weakness calls for. |

Record 'trusted input only' as a precondition if a triage or dispatch agent is ever proposed. The source is an outline entry with no content. |

One phase per session in the desktop app needs no parallel-session manager. |

Adding uv is an install for scripts that are mostly JS. |

The general principle (live paths over tests) is already implied by the traced-path rule, and no phase does prop narrowing. |

A scheduled loop that deletes fallbacks would reopen fixed defects. |

Revisit only if the repos split. |

A CI agent that edits docs needs API secrets in CI and reads untrusted PR content, and the source is a README row. |

The only new part is a deterministic build script, and no harness artifact needs one: OWNER-VIEW is mostly judgment prose with no machine-readable state source, so a script would add drift and upkeep to fix a problem nobody has seen yet. |

## G4 — L1 + L3 — effort routing, verifiers, backpressure, benchmarks (244 families, 633 cards)

| Family | Final | Claim | Cards | A: e/t/n/b/c → disp | B: e/t/n/b/c → disp | Refuter | One line |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G4-F022 | **adopt** | Single-run benchmark differences (including medium vs xhigh) cannot separate effort from run-to-run variance; treat 1-4 strict-pass gaps as noise unless repeated, ideally five runs averaged. | 3: C0252, C0691, C0632 | 2/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 2/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Survives only as a zero-cost, symmetric reading rule for this review's L1 decisions, not as a standing rule. The rule: a single-run benchmark gap of a few strict passes counts as "no detectable difference" for either effort level. When quality shows no detectable difference, cost and task transfer decide. Drop the 2/3/5-runs-per-arm quotas: they are statistically empty at this n, they protect the unmeasured xhigh status quo, and they reopen the skipped measurement pipeline. |
| G4-F023 | **adopt** | Measurement hygiene for any A/B: pin the sample set, prompt, seed and verifier/test-suite version, and never compare subset scores across runs or against the full benchmark. | 3: C0260, C0628, C0696 | 2/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 2/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Narrowed. The standing per-trial pin schema reopens the skipped "evaluation-run schema" (HR §4 line 275), and the seed cannot be pinned because agent() exposes none. What survives is a clause written into any A/B trial the review adopts: both arms read the same head SHA, the same brief text (committed to the existing evidence folder, with its sha256 in the existing manifest) and the same named inputs, and a result from a different input set is reported as not comparable. |
| G4-F025 | **adopt** | Benchmark and effort numbers decay (silent server-side drift improved the baseline more than any skill pack in a month), so re-measure when a model ships and log model/harness versions. | 2: C0675, C0337 | 2/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 2/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Re-measuring on every model release is refuted: it reopens the M00–M06/evaluation-run-schema skip, and the source shows drift under unchanged versions, which version logging cannot catch. What survives is (a) one provenance line in each evidence README the routine already writes (resolved model ids instead of aliases, `claude --version`, date), not per-output manifest fields or audits, and (b) a rule that any comparative effort trial runs its arms side by side in the same window. |
| G4-F044 | **adopt** | Sub-agent effort is not pinned by the product: the child inherits the session's thinking settings (model:inherit does not pin effort), and effort is a per-session launch parameter alongside workflow choice. | 3: C0095, C0121, C0367 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | narrowed → adopt | The proposed probe is unnecessary: the Workflow docs already say an omitted effort inherits the session's, and design §4.2/§3 already set effort on every agent() call. Only a one-line ship plan §0 rule survives: launch the per-PR reviewer and the implementers with an explicit effort taken from §3, never through a path that inherits Fable's. |
| G4-F073 | **adopt** | Use an out-of-band sensor like ast-grep because agents disable TypeScript config and ESLint rules with inline comments; verifier independence means a gate the agent cannot edit away. | 1: C0749 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | Drop ast-grep and adopt the narrow version. Extend the reviewer rule in ship plan §0 line 55 ("every changed expected value, removed assertion and new skip or xfail") so it also lists added inline suppressions and edits to gate config (ruff/mypy/import-linter/ESLint/tsconfig/CI), each with its reason, and back that list with a diff grep. This costs one sentence in the brief and reopens no deliberate skip. |
| G4-F077 | **adopt** | After opening a PR, run a bounded CI-watch/fix loop (wait, watch checks, fix PR-caused failures, rerun flaky once, stop after 3 attempts), asking approval rather than silently resolving conflicts. | 3: C0155, C0267, C0460 | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → adopt | The smaller version survives and is worth adopting. Add one line to §0 Gates: after local gates are green, if the same CI job fails on 3 consecutive pushes, stop, record it in the Decisions journal and hand it to the owner. Never rerun to green without journaling the failing log, and keep the app's PR Auto-fix off on phase PRs. Drop the flaky-rerun allowance, the owner approval for every conflict (already covered by the head-SHA re-read in §0), the 5-minute wait and --watch mechanics, and the W5 claim. The cap is cheap insurance against a spiral this repo has never had, and its compliance can be checked from outside via gh run history. |
| G4-F008 | **trial** | Encode recurring defect shapes and domain invariants as deterministic lint, typed and import rules (one new linter per anti-pattern) instead of adding prompt, memory or CLAUDE.md prose, since prompts alone cannot reach the needed precision. | 5: C0128, C0312, C0542, C0774, C0321 | 1/3/2/3/2 → trial | 1/2/2/2/1 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The broad family fails: its evidence is unmeasured, FixFlow's recorded recurring defect shapes (HANDOFF §6) and most CLAUDE.md invariants are semantic and already covered by tests, the proposed bare-catch rule would flag only 3 false positives today, the web already has no-empty, and the outbox import rule is already planned for P5b-2. What survives is one zero-dependency rule (ruff TID251 banning hmac.compare_digest outside core/security.py) plus a 10-PR count of how many confirmed defects an existing linter could express. The W1 gain is a few lines and the W2 gain is none. |
| G4-F017 | **trial** | Maintainability has no fast oracle: the cost of bad architecture shows up weeks to years later while tests answer in seconds, so fast loops reward the wrong thing and a slow-loop design audit is needed. | 4: C0353, C0597, C0770, C0841 | 0/3/2/2/2 → trial | 0/2/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Per-PR review is scoped to register ids and cannot see drift that builds up across PRs, so one audit as a trial survives. But it must exclude boundary edges (CI already catches them) and the two pre-existing ~1.95k-line service files. It also needs a refuter-gated pass bar of 2 or more owner-accepted findings, because both reviewers' bars pass trivially. |
| G4-F031 | **trial** | To qualify a new model, replay identical inputs through incumbent and candidate, diff outputs and hand-review only disagreements, which join the golden set. | 1: C0699 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | What survives is a one-off procedure inside the F034 effort trial: score medium and xhigh against Fable's adjudicated past verdicts, with an xhigh repeat as the noise floor and a check that the two arms are not agreeing on wrong answers. The general practice of qualifying a model by "only review disagreements, then grow a golden set" fails here: same-model errors are correlated, one diff mixes effort with noise, and a golden set would reopen the M00–M06 skip. |
| G4-F032 | **trial** | Gate a candidate model/effort with a relative budget (accuracy drop 0, cost at most 1.2x, p95 latency at most 1.5x), failing closed on unknown cost and repeating runs. | 1: C0705 | 2/2/2/2/3 → trial | 2/2/2/1/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | As a standing gate for choosing models or effort, this idea brings back the skipped evaluation-run schema and adds latency and cost thresholds that mean nothing here. What survives is a smaller version: a pass bar written before F034's medium-vs-xhigh trial runs, with 3 repeats per arm, results compared item by item against xhigh's own run-to-run variation, and an unknown token count counted as a fail. Without an F034 trial it should be skipped. |
| G4-F034 | **trial** | Medium effort matched or beat xhigh on strict passes for most model pairs at 2-4x lower cost, so default verifiers to medium and escalate only for named reasons. | 2: C0251, C0689 | 2/1/2/2/2 → trial | 2/1/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | What survives is narrower: a trial of medium only for non-P0 verifiers that now run at high or unspecified effort (review-workflow refuters and judges, the per-PR reviewer), settled by prospective paired shadow runs. The data is one run of code-generation passes, and core passes favour xhigh 2 of 3. §3 already names the xhigh exceptions, and the replay corpus both reviewers rely on is not committed. |
| G4-F068 | **trial** | Scratch files, tests and generated copies should count against quality because leftover garbage must be measured when humans are not reading the code. | 1: C0257 | 1/3/2/1/3 → adopt | 2/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Narrowed: the benchmark's garbage-scoring idea doesn't transfer, because untracked files never reach a squash-merged repo and committed files are already in the reviewed diff. Keep only a free close-time check, worktree clean plus evidence manifest complete, trialled for missed intended files (like the uncommitted split-review-input tool) rather than for slop. |
| G4-F081 | **trial** | Review-then-fix loops can themselves create slop: pedantic Opus 5 reviewers made a one-table migration balloon, so cap rounds and require reviewers to cite a failing behaviour. | 1: C0695 | 1/3/0/1/3 → already-done | 1/3/2/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | A smaller version survives. Add one line to the per-PR reviewer brief: a finding outside the named register ids blocks only if it states a failing condition (the §4.5 W2 rule applied to PR review); anything else goes into the PR journal as a non-blocking note. Count review rounds instead of capping them, because the anecdote's cause (a pedantic prompt) is absent and Fable already gates fixes. |
| G4-F084 | **trial** | Cross-model review (e.g. Codex reviewing Claude, queued overnight, via a bash script or one CLI calling the other) finds and fixes small issues and raises trust, but does not remove the review bottleneck. | 3: C0454, C0568, C0642 | 1/2/3/2/1 → trial | 1/2/3/1/1 → trial | narrowed → trial | Narrowed to one bounded, owner-approved trial: a single Codex review pass on a PR diff, aimed at code that Opus both writes and reviews, and tested against a second Opus pass rather than the existing reviewer. The overnight 15-prompt loop with compaction and the saved workflow are dropped. The evidence is anecdotal, there is no Codex CLI on the machine, and HANDOFF §6 shows the earlier misses came from scope and process, which a second same-vendor pass already caught. |
| G4-F086 | **trial** | Verify that new tests fail on pre-patch code (restore original tests, run the agent's tests against the base commit) to catch tests that test nothing; make it a mechanical script, not self-reported. | 5: C0573, C0781, C0797, C0762, C0874 | 1/2/2/3/2 → trial | 1/3/1/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | Only a smaller trial survives. The fail-before principle is already plan §0 Routing (HR §4 row 261), and the cards have no results. A naive run on the base commit would certify tests that only die on ImportError or a 404. The trial is a script for register-id defect fixes only: it requires an assertion failure on base and a pass on head, and checks base tests against head for weakened expectations. It runs serially with the Docker gate. It covers one reviewer claim, not W2 as a whole. |
| G4-F088 | **trial** | Check for duplicated state (second source of truth) and representable illegal states, because green tests don't show it: 12.5% of 200 runs added a second source of truth and about 40% kept a separate ordering vector. | 2: C0477, C0478 | 2/2/2/2/3 → adopt | 2/2/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Narrowed to a trial, not an adopt: one line in the Opus reviewer's refute brief, as an eighth item on the defect-shape checklist planned by KNOWLEDGE-STORE-DESIGN ruling 6 and never in CLAUDE.md. It should ask the reviewer to name, for any new stored copy or flag, which source is authoritative and what keeps the copy consistent, and to check whether the new types or states can represent an illegal state such as `ready` without a completion or unknown read as none. It must count dual records that CLAUDE.md already justifies as known. The cited data measures how often agents introduce the defect, not whether a reviewer line catches it, and it bears on L3 only. |
| G4-F089 | **trial** | Give reviewers specific named anti-patterns from the owner's past pain ('would you debug this at 2am') rather than a generic maintainability review or blind refactor, which models cannot judge. | 3: C0600, C0793, C0501 | 1/3/1/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/0/1/3 → already-done | narrowed → trial | Only a smaller version survives. Take the seven HANDOFF §6 shapes out of KS-4, correct item 2's login-lock example to match the CLAUDE.md invariant, and put them in the SHIP-PLAN §0 reviewer brief with a required per-shape line in each verdict. Run that as a trial. G4-A's path pointer is already in CONTEXT §5.1 and carries the wrong example. G4-B's "already-done" is not true, because the checklist is parked behind the unbuilt store track. Blind refactor phases do not exist in the plan. |
| G4-F140 | **trial** | Humans must keep reading the code (at least on money, auth and lifecycle paths): an unread lights-off software factory quietly rots and ends in an unfixable 3am outage, and specs or plans are not a substitute for reading. | 16: C0881, C0906, C1215, C0936, C0941, C1261 … | 1/2/2/2/2 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | What survives is narrower than "read every line". The owner reads only the hunks the reviewer marks as touching invariants, only on PRs in §0's Opus-owned list, recorded against the head SHA, trialled on 3 PRs. The broad claim rests on HumanLayer anecdotes without numbers, from a team with no model reviewer, which FixFlow already has. The W1 benefit is illusory and the W2 benefit is partial. |
| G4-F152 | **trial** | Every plan step should carry its exact, automated verification command and expected result (manual curl steps converted to tests), with rules and acceptance criteria grounded in the plan rather than invented at review time. | 3: C1233, C1419, C1841 | 1/3/1/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Narrowed to a trial. Two of the family's three parts already exist: reviewer refute lists come from the plan's register ids, and P1 already turns its manual checks into automated tests. Register-id boxes already carry fail-before/pass-after tests (SHIP-PLAN §0 Routing). What survives is one line per tick for the non-register boxes (CI and harness gates, docs, deploy steps): the verifying command, its expected result and a negative run for each gate. I am overriding reviewer B's adopt to a trial because the evidence is anecdotal and the routine has never run. |
| G4-F157 | **trial** | Wrap test/build/lint commands so success prints one tick (or nothing) and only failures dump filtered, fail-fast output; deterministic wrappers, not the model, decide what is truncated, since flooding context with passing tests makes agents hallucinate. | 8: C1254, C1575, C1581, C1583, C1886, C1522 … | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | The core idea, a deterministic wrapper that writes full logs to a file and returns one line per gate, is already done: Appendix A's g() is mandated for backend gates by ship plan §0. The one-tick output would erase the skip-aware signal, and fail-fast contradicts the every-gate design. Only a small version survives as a trial: apply the same g() discipline (one EXIT line plus the runner's pass/skip counts, a failure excerpt instead of a bare tail) to the natively run web and mobile gates, run by the command subagent rather than Fable. |
| G4-F181 | **trial** | Keep a private journal of git SHAs and prompts for problems that cost you a week, and re-run them as a personal benchmark on every new model or workflow (Fable 5 failed one by keying on a non-unique id). | 3: C0993, C1019, C0994 | 1/3/2/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/2 → trial | narrowed → trial | The standing "week-cost journal re-run on every new model" is refuted by its own source: the one reported case never told models apart, and Dex says one-shot is a poor test in the same video. A smaller version survives as a trial: about 5 known-answer fixtures from FixFlow's closed defects, run repeatedly with blind grading as the fixture set of the L1 effort trial, with no standing infrastructure and with the reviewers' noise-prone keep rule fixed. |
| G4-F185 | **trial** | Models over-engineer by default (unrequested abstractions, impossible-case handling, huge line counts), so state scope and explicit non-goals in the plan and check complexity/over-engineering in review. | 3: C1373, C1770, C1845 | 1/3/1/1/3 → trial | 1/3/1/1/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Only the reviewer-brief half survives, as a trial. The plan-side "not doing" section already exists as the enumerated Scope, the "Backlog in these files" and Size lines, and the "ruled out" journal. The evidence is anecdotal, so B's adopt is too strong. The check must also exclude CLAUDE.md-protected defensive code, or it invites the reviewer to "simplify" invariants away. |
| G4-F186 | **trial** | Run the review as a separate step in a fresh context with a fixed list of known anti-patterns and a plan-vs-implementation deviation report, rather than buying a separate review product. | 2: C1282, C1416 | 1/3/1/2/3 → trial | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | The fresh-context reviewer and the anti-pattern list already exist (§0 Routing; CLAUDE.md invariants, loaded by every subagent). Only the one-section deviation report against Plan @ sha survives: it is the one outside check on ticked boxes and unrecorded additions. It runs as a trial rather than an adopt because its evidence is anecdotal and the reviewer has never run. |

| G5-F024 | **trial** | Prompt-injection risk is the lethal trifecta (untrusted content, private data, external communication); removing any one leg (cut prod DB access, network sandbox, guardrail) fixes it. | 1: C1222 | 1/2/2/2/3 → adopt | 1/3/2/2/3 → adopt | narrowed → trial | Narrowed. The dispatcher landing is refuted (no LLM in the product), and so is "a guardrail fixes it". The reviewers' brief-sentence rule changes nothing: Railway and GitHub CLI credentials sit in the user profile and subagents run in bypass mode. What survives is a trial of a structural cut of the private-data leg: a restricted research agent type with deny rules for any context that reads web/transcript/third-party text, plus no WebFetch/WebSearch in sessions that hold prod credentials. It is cheap, but it buys nothing on W1-W5/L1-L3, and it works only if A shows the restrictions are enforced on workflow subagents. |
